L767: Abbreviations: AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; AUClast, area under the concentration-time curve to the last quantifiable time-point; F, female; M, male; NOAEL, no-observed-adverse-effect level
L768: Across the nonclinical studies included in Table 51, both M1a and M1b metabolites generally demonstrated exposures exceeding 0.5-fold relative to the clinical exposure at the 20mg dose, a threshold typically warranted to achieve adequate nonclinical assessment for disproportionate metabolites. The exception was M1a exposure in male animals in carcinogenicity studies, where exposures were limited to 0.2-fold in the 6-month transgenic mouse study and 0.3-fold in the 2-year rat study. This lower exposure was attributed to two factors: cardiac toxicities associated with aficamten that limited the high dose selection, and the inherently lower M1a levels present in rodent species relative to humans, after aficamten administration. Given the higher maximum tolerated dose of aficamten in females, M1a exposure in females reached 0.5-fold relative to clinical exposure without tumor findings in the 2-year rat carcinogenicity study. Mechanistically the M1a metabolite is formed through first-pass metabolism by P450-mediated hydroxylation and is structurally very similar to aficamten, which was negative for tumor signals in 6-month and 2-year rodent carcinogenicity studies.
L769: M1a is pharmacologically inactive, and nonclinical rodent studies achieving exposures above 0.5-fold (including the 13-week rat study) showed no proliferative or preneoplastic findings of concern. The 2-year rat study, which reached 0.5-fold exposure, similarly demonstrated no concerning signals. Furthermore, M1a was determined to be non-genotoxic based on a comprehensive battery of assays and weight-of-evidence evaluation. Collectively, these data indicate minimal carcinogenic risk for this metabolite, therefore, we conclude that a dedicated carcinogenicity study is not warranted.
L770: 13.1.2.3. Drug-Drug Interactions
L771: Aficamten was not a substrate for OATP1B1/B3, MDR1/BCRP, or P-gp in vitro. In a transporter panel inhibition screen, aficamten and its major metabolites had no significant effects on the activity of OAT1, OCT2, OCT3, or OATP1B3. The IC50 for aficamten against OAT3, OATP1B1, MATE1, and MATE2-K were 12, 71, 36, and 83μM, respectively. In vitro CYP phenotyping, induction, and inhibition studies using recombinant CYP proteins, human liver microsomes, human hepatocytes, or a human microLiver model were performed. Results from the studies collectively suggest that CYPs 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, and 3A4 contribute to metabolic clearance of aficamten in humans. Aficamten did not significantly inhibit any CYP enzyme in in vitro assays. Based on thresholds described in the FDA guidance for industry Clinical Drug Interaction Studies—Cytochrome P450 Enzyme- and Transporter-Mediated Drug Interactions (January 2020),5 the in vivo risk for inhibition by aficamten is low.
L772: 13.1.3. General Toxicology
L773: Repeat oral dose toxicology studies up to 26 weeks in rats and 39 weeks in dogs were conducted to support the chronic oral administration of aficamten intended for clinical use. Both species are considered pharmacologically relevant given the high conservation of the cardiac myosin motor between species and based on in vitro and single-dose in vivo pharmacology studies. Exposure
L774: 5 Guidances are updated periodically. For the most recent version of a guidance, check the FDA guidance web page at https://www.fda.gov/regulatory-information/search-fdaguidance-documents.
L775: multiples were estimated based on systemic exposure to total and unbound (free) aficamten (AUC and C_{max}) at the maximum recommended human clinical dose of 20 mg.
L776: Repeat dosing at the clinical exposure based on free AUC (5-times greater than clinical exposure based on total AUC) for up to 6-months in rats was well tolerated. In shorter 28-day and 13-week rat studies, dosing at 2-fold greater than clinical free AUC level (8-times greater than clinical total AUC) was tolerated, while dilated cardiomyopathy and premature deaths were associated with dosing at 4-fold greater than clinical free AUC (14-times greater than clinical total AUC).
L777: Similarly, in the 39-week dog study dosing to the clinical free AUC level caused no toxicities (0.25-times the clinical total AUC). However, a dose level 2.5-fold greater than clinical free AUC in dogs (0.6-times the clinical total AUC) had reversible increased heart weight. In a shorter 13-week dog study, dosing at 4-fold clinical free AUC (at clinical total AUC) was associated with cardiomyocyte necrosis and premature death. Taken together, the repeat-dose toxicology studies demonstrate dose and duration-dependent cardiac toxicities in healthy animals given daily aficamten.
L778: FDA Assessment
L779: The heart was identified as the primary organ of toxicity in both species with repeated aficamten administrations. Cardiac toxicities culminating in heart failure resulting from exaggerated pharmacology of the drug were observed in both rats and dogs in a dose- and duration-dependent fashion. Drug exposures causing cardiac toxicity for both species were &gt;2-fold and &gt;3-fold the maximum clinical exposures based on free AUC and free C_{max}, respectively. Cardiac toxicities in dogs were evident at clinical exposure based on total AUC and 2-times clinical exposure based on total C_{max}. In rats, cardiac toxicity occurred at 14-times clinical exposure based on total AUC and 32-times clinical exposure based on total C_{max}.
L780: No adverse effects for either species were observed with chronic dosing at clinical exposure based on free AUC and up to 3-fold based on free C_{max}. No adverse findings occurred in dogs at exposures less than clinical total AUC and total C_{max}. For rats, no adverse findings occurred when dosing up to 5-times total AUC and 9-times total C_{max}. Hypercontractility is one of the pathological hallmarks of hypertrophic cardiomyopathy, and the indicated patient population would likely tolerate a reduction in cardiac contractility to a greater extent than animals or humans with 'normal' cardiac contractility. As such, while the nonclinical toxicity studies clearly demonstrate that aficamten is capable of inhibiting myosin/actin interactions with a narrow safety margin and, in excess, can precipitate heart failure, a broader therapeutic window may be present in the intended patient population.

L814: 13.1.4. Genetic Toxicology
L815: A GLP-compliant Ames assay and a GLP combined in vivo mammalian erythrocyte micronucleus test and alkaline comet assay in rats were conducted in accordance with the guidelines outlined in the International Council for Harmonisation (ICH) guidance for industry, S1(R1) S1B(R1) Addendum to S1B Testing for Carcinogenicity of Pharmaceuticals (November 2022b). Aficamten was negative for genotoxicity and clastogenicity in both systems. GLP-compliant Ames assays and in vitro micronucleus assays were conducted for the two major metabolites of aficamten, with a follow-up combined in vivo micronucleus assay and comet assay conducted for metabolite M1a. Collectively, the metabolites were also negative for genotoxicity and clastogenicity. See Table 57 for details.
L816: 13.1.4.1. Carcinogenesis, Mutagenesis, and Impairment of Fertility
L817: Aficamten was negative in the bacterial mutagenesis (Ames) assay, an in vivo rat micronucleus study and in an in vivo comet assay.
L818: Aficamten was not associated with tumor formation in both a 6-month transgenic mouse study at clinical exposure and a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study up to 2-fold and 4-fold clinical AUCunbound for males and females, respectively.
L819: Aficamten had no effects on fertility, mating, or reproductive assessments up to 6 mg/kg/day in females and 3 mg/kg/day in males.
L820: The genetic toxicology studies conducted for aficamten, as well as its major metabolites, are summarized in Table 58.

L825: 13.1.4.1.1. Carcinogenicity
L826: GLP-compliant carcinogenicity studies of 6 months in transgenic rasH2 mice (Study Nonclin-0306) and 2 years in Sprague Dawley rats (Study Nonclin-0248) were conducted after Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee (ECAC) concurrence regarding dose selection.
L827: Aficamten was associated with an apparent dose-dependent trend for bronchioloalveolar adenomas in male transgenic mice in the 6-month study with dosing up to 2 mg/kg/day corresponding to a clinical exposure margin of 2-fold based on free AUC (0.6-fold based on total AUC). The trend and pairwise comparisons between vehicle treated and aficamten treated animals were not statistically significant based on ECAC statistical criteria. In a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study, low survivability at the high dose for both sexes necessitated early stoppage of dosing and study termination after ECAC concurrence. No aficamten-related hyperplastic or neoplastic microscopic findings were noted for any organ in the 2-year rat study, up to 2 mg/kg/day in males and 3 mg/kg/day in females corresponding to clinical exposure margins of 2.5-fold and 4-fold based on free AUC, respectively (8-fold and 15-fold based on male and female total AUC, respectively).
L828: 13.1.4.2. Reproductive Toxicology
L829: GLP-compliant reproductive toxicology studies including a fertility and early embryonic development study in rats, embryofetal development studies in rats and rabbits, and a pre- and post-natal development study in rats were conducted with aficamten. Details of these studies are summarized below in Table 59.

L847: Study Number/Title: Nonclin-0161/CK-3773274: An Oral Embryofetal Development Study in Rats (GLP)
L848: Key Study Findings
L849: • Statistically significant increased maternal mean absolute heart weight was observed at 6 and 9 mg/kg/day (up to 13% increase over controls).• Maternal toxicity characterized by decreased body weight gain, reduced food consumption, and decreased terminal body weight at 9 mg/kg/day, which reached statistical significance at sporadic intervals throughout the study, were adverse (up to 30% reduced food consumption, 64% reduced body weight gain).• The mean total fetuses per litter was reduced at 9 mg/kg/day, with mean postimplantation loss of ~59% and a mean early resorption rate of ~7%. Three incidences of total litter loss, and one incidence of abnormal placenta (pale foci) occurred at the same dose. See Table 63.• Surviving fetuses from the 9 mg/kg/day group had statistically significant reduction in weight compared to controls, with increased incidence of kinked and short tails. See Table 63.• The NOAEL from this study was 6 mg/kg/day for both maternal and fetal toxicity with systemic exposures of 93,900 and 1,502 ng·h/mL for total and free AUC, respectively, and 10,400 and 166 ng/mL for total and free Cmax, respectively. Clinical safety margins based on total and free AUC are 12X and 3.5X, respectively. Clinical safety margins based on total and free Cmax are 32X and 9X, respectively.

L866: Study Number/Title: Nonclin-0199/CK-3773274: An Oral Embryofetal Development Study in Rabbits
L867: Key Study Findings
L868: • There were dose-dependent reductions in maternal body weight gain, which were not associated with decreased food consumption between Days 7 to 10 postcoital for all groups (7, 17, 27% reduction compared to controls), which persisted for the high dose group through Day 20 postcoital (overall body weight reduction of 11% compared to controls).
• Statistically significant increased late resorptions occurred for the mid dose only, with mean postimplantation loss occurring at a rate (10.5%) toward the high end of the historical control values (1.3 to 10.7%). See Table 67.
• Fetal cardiovascular malformations including dilated aorta, absent ventricular septation, misshapen heart, retroesophageal subclavian artery, and truncus arteriosus occurred in the mid-dose group only (10 mg/kg/day) at incidences exceeding the historical control range. Truncus arteriosus was found in litters from the high dose group (20/15 mg/kg/day) at a rate (0.6%) toward the high end of the historical control range (0.61%). See Table 67.
• The maternal and embryofetal NOAEL doses were 10 mg/kg/day and 15 mg/kg/day, respectively, with exposure margins below clinical exposure at the maximum recommended human dose; doses higher than 15 mg/kg/day were not tolerated in rabbits.
• The embryofetal NOAEL is based on the following:
            
– A slight numerical increase in the incidence (litters) of cardiovascular malformations above concurrent and historical controls was observed in the mid-dose group.
– The imbalance was not dose-related and findings were absent or had similar incidence between control and high-dose groups.
– We considered whether the four litters lost (on Days 7 to 9 post-coital) at the 20/15 mg/kg high dose may have the potential to confound the analysis.
                    
▪ Importantly, the missing litters were clearly due to maternal toxicity and death and were not related to embryofetal toxicity (per se). Furthermore, postimplantation loss and total number of fetuses (per litter) in surviving females was similar to controls. These data indicate it was unlikely that maternal deaths confounded our analysis.
L869: In conclusion, the incidence of malformations was deemed to be similar across dose groups, similar to the concurrent and/or historical control levels, lacking in any apparent dose-response, and unlikely to have been caused by lost litters (due to maternal deaths, not abortion/resorption),
L870: and thus could not be attributed to aficamtene, despite a signal reported for the first-in-class cardiac myosin inhibitor, mavacamtene.

L873: Source: Nonclin-0199Study
L874: Table 67. Observations and Results: Nonclin-0199Study
L875: EndpointsMajor Findings



Endpoints with notable observations
Maternal toxicity
Three animals in the high dose group (20 mg/kg/day) died by postcoital Day 8, with an additional one on postcoital Day 9 after reducing the dose to 15 mg/kg/day. One animal given 10 mg/kg/day was found dead on postcoital Day 14, with preceding signs of decreased activity, abnormal breathing, and muzzle discharge. In the low dose group, one animal was humanely euthanized on postcoital Day 19 having been found with reduced activity, thin appearance, and abdominal distension. Reductions in body weight gain were reported for all dose groups early in the study, without accompanying increased cardiac weights.
Offspring toxicity
A slight imbalance in fetal cardiovascular malformations was noted at the mid-dose at a rate slightly above the historical control range, with no apparent dose-dependent trend. The relation to aficamtene is questionable given the minor increase above controls, overall low total incidence, and lack of dose-dependence.


Endpoints without notable observationsMacroscopic pathology, organ weights, pregnancy rate, uterine examination, external fetal examination, mean fetal weights

L889: Study Number/Title: Nonclin-0360/An Oral (Gavage) Study of the Effects of CK-3773274 on Pre- and Postnatal Development in Rats

L891: • At the high dose, 6 mg/kg/day, mean maternal heart weight was increased relative to controls by 22%. L908: 13.1.5. Other Toxicology/Specialized Studies
L909: Major metabolites (M1a and M1b) of aficamten were characterized in a non-GLP 14-day repeat dose study in rats, GLP Ames assays, and GLP in vivo and in vitro genetic toxicology studies. In addition, aficamten was evaluated in a phototoxicity assay in vitro, and skin sensitization and corrosion assays. In general, the major aficamten metabolites were well tolerated, not pharmacodynamically active in vivo, and not genotoxic or clastogenic at clinically relevant levels. Aficamten was not irritating or corrosive to the eye or skin in any study conducted. The results of these studies are summarized below:
L910: 13.1.5.1. Metabolites
L911: Study Number/Title: CK-3834282 and CK-3834283/A 14-Day Oral Dose Tolerability Study in Sprague Dawley Rat (Nonclin-0221) (Non-GLP)
L912: • Major aficamten metabolites were administered separately by oral gavage to rats for 14 consecutive days at 50 mg/kg/day.• No remarkable clinical observations or effects on heart weight were found for either metabolite up to Cmax levels of 13,933 ng/mL for males and 28,032 ng/mL for females for M1a, and Cmax levels of 45,197 ng/mL for males and 62,663 ng/mL for females for M1b. AUC0-24h values for males and females were 35,679 and 59,799 ng·h/mL for M1a, and 215,085 and 287,778 ng·h/mL for M1b, respectively.• The major aficamten metabolites were not pharmacologically active in vivo up to clinical exposure multiples &gt;100-times based on steady state Cmax, and &gt;10-times based on steady state AUC from clinical Study CY 6021 at an aficamten dose of 20 mg.
L913: Study Number/Title: CK-3834282/Bacterial Reverse Mutation Assay (Study Nonclin-0231) and CK-3834283: Bacterial Reverse Mutation Assay (Study Nonclin-0234)
L914: • Bacterial tester strains were grown with metabolite M1a or M1b up to 5000 µg/plate in the presence or absence of rat liver S9.• Test-article precipitation was observed for both M1a and M1b at 3,333 µg/plate and 5000 µg/plate, respectively.• M1a did not induce an increase in revertant bacterial colonies up to 3,333 µg/plate. Toxicity for M1a was noted at ≥3,333 µg/plate in the absence of S9.
L915: • Growth of S. typhimurium strain TA98 with M1b at 100 µg/mL induced a 1.8X increase in revertant colonies compared to controls in the absence of S9, but there was no dose-dependent increase in revertant colonies above that dose.• The Ames assay was negative for both M1a and M1b under the conditions of these studies.
L916: Study Number/Title: CK-3834282/In Vitro Mammalian Cell Micronucleus Assay in TK6 Cells (Study Nonclin-0233) and CK-3834283: In Vitro Mammalian Cell Micronucleus Assay in TK6 Cells (Study Nonclin-0236)
L917: • TK6 lymphoblasts were treated with M1a or M1b in the presence and absence of rat liver S9 up to 353 µg/mL for 4 hours, or in the absence of S9 for 27 hours.• A statistically significant increase in micronuclei was observed at M1a concentrations \geq 200 µg/mL in the absence of S9 after 27 hours of treatment compared to controls. The value of the micronuclei induction at the highest dose (1.55%) was only slightly above the maximum historical control value (1.5%).• Under the conditions of the study, M1a was considered positive for inducing micronuclei without S9 activation at exposure equivalent to \geq 4000-times clinical C_{max} at the maximum recommended human dose.• No statistically significant increases in micronuclei induction were found for M1b under any condition up to 353 µg/mL in the absence of S9 for 27 hours.
L918: Study Number/Title: CK-3834282/Combined In Vivo Mammalian Peripheral Blood Erythrocyte Micronucleus Test and Alkaline Comet Assay in Rat (Study Nonclin-0276)
L919: • Vehicle, positive controls, or M1a up to 2000 mg/kg/day was administered to rats by oral gavage for 4 consecutive days after which micronucleated reticulocytes in the periphery were counted using flow cytometry. The mean percentage of micronucleated cells from M1a treated animals at all doses was within historical negative control range.• In the same study, hepatocytes were used for a comet assay to assess DNA damage. All M1a treated groups had Tail DNA values within historical negative control range.• Positive controls induced increases in the mean percentage of micronucleated reticulocytes and Tail DNA in the comet assay, verifying the validity of the test system.• M1a was not clastogenic in vivo up to 2900-times clinical C_{max} and 440-times clinical AUC at the maximum recommended clinical dose for aficamten. The in vitro plasma protein binding of M1a was similar between rat and human. Although the major metabolite M1a without metabolic activation was positive for inducing micronuclei in human lymphoblasts in vitro, it was not mutagenic in a bacterial Ames assay. M1a administered to rats in vivo for 4 days did not induce micronuclei in circulating reticulocytes and did not cause DNA damage in hepatocytes at a dose &gt; 1000-times the clinical exposure based on C_{max} and &gt; 400-times based on AUC, similar to the exposure which did induce micronuclei in lymphoblasts in vitro. Taken together, the weight of evidence suggests that the M1a metabolite is unlikely to be mutagenic or clastogenic at clinically relevant doses of aficamten.
L920: 13.1.5.2. Skin and Eye Irritation Studies

All studies were valid and were conducted according to GLP and/or Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development guidelines.

Nonclin-0108A GLP Neutral Red Uptake Phototoxicity Assay of CK-3773274 in BALB/c 3T3 Mouse FibroblastsNegative, aficamten did not induce additional cytotoxicity above the negative control when ultraviolet radiation was applied over a range of concentrations up to 32 µg/mL.

L925: 13.1.5.2.1. Impurities/Degradants
L926: Impurities for aficamten included a non-mutagenic process impurity ( (b) (4) ) and four impurities containing structural alerts ( (b) (4) ) for mutagenicity. One of the mutagenic impurities was used in an Ames assay for read-through data to qualify the others. Summary reviews of toxicology studies using these impurities are below:
L927: Study Number/Title: (b) (4) : A 13-Week Oral Gavage Toxicity Study in Rats (Study Nonclin-0440)
L928: • An aficamten process impurity (b) (4) was administered daily to rats by oral gavage for 13 weeks at 0, 0.00625, or 0.0125 mg/kg/day to qualify the impurity at a specification of not more than (b) (4) % in the aficamten drug substance.• Mean body weight for rats administered (b) (4) was modestly reduced in males without a clear dose-dependent trend (-11% and -8% compared to controls for the low and high dose, respectively). Other clinical pathology findings during the dosing period were minor or limited to only one sex.• At terminal necropsy, females from the high dose group had increased mean pituitary gland weight (plus 23% increase relative to brain weight compared to controls) accompanied by diffuse pars distalis hyperplasia, moderately reduced number of corpora lutea, increased vaginal mucification, and minimal mammary gland hyperplasia. The findings suggest a disruption of the endocrine system in females, however, functionally there were no accompanying disruptions to the estrus cycle.• The findings were considered non-adverse up to (b) (4) mg/kg/day for females and up to (b) (4) mg/kg/day for males.• Process impurity (b) (4) is being limited to not more than (b) (4) % aficamten.

L936: Results/Conclusion
L937: We concur with the Applicant's position that CK-3943113 was not mutagenic under any conditions used for this study. Revertant colony number slightly exceeded the 98% tolerance threshold for negative control data at 50 µg/plate and 158 µg/plate with S9 for strains TA98 and TA1537 respectively. However, revertant colony number did not exceed the ≥2-fold increase cutoff above negative controls for any strain to be considered positive, and there was no dose-dependent increase in revertant colonies for any strain.

L944: In a mid-cycle communication dated 04/03/2025, the Agency suggested that the Applicant either conduct Ames testing to assess mutagenic potential or control the impurities as Class 3 per ICH M7. The Applicant agreed to conduct a GLP Ames assay for one of the four impurities (b) (4) (b) (4) and use the results as read-through data for the others. The Applicant could not synthesize and isolate sufficient (b) (4), so the Ames assay was done using (b) (4) instead, and the Agency agreed with this approach per consultation with CDER Computational Toxicology Consultation Service (CTCS). The review for the Ames study using (b) (4) is included below.

L960: 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L961: 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L962: Plasma Protein Binding and Plasma Partitioning
L963: Plasma protein binding of aficamten in humans was assessed at 0.5, 2, and 10µM aficamten and metabolites (CK-3834282 and CK-3834283) (Study Nonclin-0017, Nonclin-0249, and Nonclin-0124). The mean plasma protein binding was \geq 88.8\%, \geq 80.5\%, and \geq 92.5\% across concentration levels for aficamten, CK-3834282, and CK-3834283, respectively. No apparent concentration dependency of plasma protein binding was observed. No apparent differences in plasma protein
L964: binding of aficamten was observed between clinical study samples from subjects with normal hepatic function and moderate HI (Study CY 6017).
L965: Blood cell partitioning of aficamten and metabolites (CK-3834282 and CK-3834283) was evaluated in human whole blood at concentrations of 1 and 10\mu\text{M} (Studies Nonclin-0032 and Nonclin-0201). Whole blood-to-plasma ratio was approximately 0.9, 0.8, and 0.7 across concentration levels for aficamten, CK-3834282, and CK-3834283, respectively. No apparent concentration dependency of partitioning was observed.
L966: In Vitro Hepatic Metabolism
L967: Incubation of aficamten (1\mu\text{M}) with human liver microsomes and recombinant CYP isoenzymes showed that aficamten was metabolized by CYP2D6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4 but not by CYP1A2, CYP2B6, and CYP2C8 (Study Nonclin-0029). Formation of the metabolite CK-3834282 was completely inhibited in the presence of a pan-CYP inhibitor (i.e., 1-aminobenzotriazole) but not in the presence of individual CYP2D6, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4-specific inhibitors, supporting that aficamten is metabolized by multiple CYP isoenzymes. The fraction of aficamten metabolized (f_m) by CYP2D6 was evaluated using Hurelhuman™ hepatocyte microliver model and estimated to be 52.1% in pooled hepatocytes.
L968: In Vitro CYP Inhibition and Induction
L969: CYP Inhibition
L970: Aficamten (0.046 to 100\mu\text{M}) exhibited no direct inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 (i.e., half-maximal inhibitory concentration [\text{IC}_{50}] &gt;100\mu\text{M}) when evaluated in human liver microsomes (Study Nonclin-0033). Time-dependent inhibition was not observed for any of these CYP isoforms when incubated with 0.046 to 100\mu\text{M} aficamten (Study Nonclin-0052). In a follow-up study, aficamten was potentially a weak metabolism-dependent inhibitor of CYP2C9 because minimal metabolism-dependent inhibition (i.e., approximately 22%) was observed at the highest test concentration of 50\mu\text{M}; the \text{IC}_{50} of this inhibition was &gt;50\mu\text{M} (Study Nonclin-0160). The Applicant reports that test concentration of 50\mu\text{M} is approximately 580 times higher than the maximal unbound plasma concentration of aficamten at steady-state (\text{I}_{\text{max,u}}) of 0.054\mu\text{M} based on a maximal total exposure in humans (\text{I}_{\text{max,ss}}) of 0.972\mu\text{M} occurring with dosing aficamten 20-mg QD (Trial CY 6031) and the human unbound fraction in plasma (f_{u,p}) of 0.056 (Study CY 6017).
L971: Aficamten metabolites, CK-3834282 and CK-3834283 (0.046 to 100\mu\text{M}) exhibited no direct inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4midazolam (\text{IC}_{50}&gt;100 \mu\text{M}) when evaluated in human liver microsomes (Study Nonclin-0126). The \text{IC}_{50} of CK-3834282 was &gt;100, 22.2, 88.7, and 76.8\mu\text{M} for CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4testosterone, respectively. The \text{IC}_{50} of CK-3834283 was 65.3, 19.5, 96.9, and 87.3\mu\text{M} for CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, and CYP3A4testosterone, respectively. Time-dependent inhibition was not observed for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, and CYP3A4 with either metabolite.
L972: CYP Induction
L973: Aficamten (0.3, 1, 3, 10, 25, and 50\mu\text{M}) did not exhibit potential to induce CYP2B6 or CYP3A4 but potentially induced CYP1A2 when evaluated by quantitation of messenger ribonucleic acid (mRNA) expression in human primary hepatocytes (Study Nonclin-0016). The potential of
L974: aficamten and its metabolites (CK-3834282 and CK-3834283) for clinically relevant induction of CYP1A2 was evaluated using the “basic mRNA fold-change method” in accordance with the ICH guidance for industry M12 Drug Interaction Studies (August 2024). The lowest measured test concentration that resulted in a \geq 2-fold increase in CYP1A2 mRNA expression relative to vehicle control was approximately 926, 245, and 41 times the I_{max,u} and C_{max,u} for aficamten, CK-3834282, and CK-3834283, respectively, at the highest proposed therapeutic dose (aficamten 20-mg QD) (Study Nonclin-0494). Therefore, aficamten and its metabolites are not predicted to be potential in vivo CYP1A2 inducers in humans and a confirmatory clinical DDI study was not conducted to evaluate this potential.
L975: In Vitro Interaction With Transporters
L976: In vitro, aficamten (0.25 to 25 \mu\text{M}) was not a substrate of breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP), P-gp, organic anion transporting polypeptide (OATP) 1B1, or OATP1B3 when evaluated in Madin-Darby canine kidney-II (MDCK-II) cells (Study Nonclin-0009).
L977: The potential for aficamten and its metabolites (CK-3834282 and CK-3834283) to inhibit efflux (BCRP and P-gp) and solute-linked carrier (i.e., organic cation transporter [OCT]2, OCT3, organic anion transporter [OAT]1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, multidrug and toxin extrusion proteins [MATE]1, and MATE2-K) transporters was evaluated in transporter-transfected MDCK-II cells (Studies Nonclin-0008, Nonclin-0127, and Nonclin-0169). Aficamten (50 \mu\text{M}) did not significantly inhibit BCRP-, OAT1-, OATP1B3-, OCT2, or OCT3-mediated transport. Clinically relevant inhibition of OATP1B1, OAT3, MATE1, and MATE2-K by aficamten is considered unlikely because the R values, calculated as [I_{max,u}]/IC_{50} for OCT2, OAT3, MATE1, and MATE2-K or as 1^+ \text{ (unbound fraction in plasma } [f_{u,p}] \times \text{estimated maximum plasma inhibitor concentration at the inlet to the liver } [I_{in,max}]) / IC_{50} for OATP1B1, determined using the highest proposed therapeutic dose (aficamten 20-mg QD) were below the thresholds for potential risk of 0.1 and 1.1, respectively. The ratio for unbound hepatic inlet concentration (C_{max,u,inlet}/K_i or IC_{50} (OATP1B1 and OAT3) and C_{max,u}/K_i or IC_{50} (MATE1 and MATE2-K) determined at the highest therapeutic dose of aficamten 20-mg QD for both cases was below the threshold for potential risk of 0.1 and 0.02, respectively, per the ICH guidance for industry M12 Drug Interaction Studies (August 2024).
L978: However, the potential for clinically relevant inhibition of P-gp by aficamten could not be excluded because the intestinal luminal concentration (calculated as \text{dose}/250 \text{ mL})/IC_{50} was 48.8, which is above the threshold for potential risk of 10. Consequently, a clinical DDI study was conducted to evaluate the effect of aficamten on the pharmacokinetics of a sensitive P-gp substrate, dabigatran (Study CY 6014, Cohort 3).
L979: In addition, the Applicant reports that aficamten metabolites, CK-3834282 and CK-383283 are not a sensitive substrate of drug transporters. CK-3834282 and CK-383283 are not expected to inhibit BCRP, OCT2, OCT3, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, MATE1, or MATE2-K drug transporters at clinically relevant concentrations.
L980: 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L981: 14.2.1. Study CY 6011: Single- and Multiple-Dose Pharmacokinetics in Healthy Subjects and Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics in CYP2D6 Poor Metabolizers
L982: Study Design
L983: This was a Phase 1, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, multipart, single- and multiple-ascending dose (MAD) study to determine the safety and tolerability of aficamten in healthy subjects. This study was conducted in 5 parts.
L984: Part 1 (SAD) and Part 2 (MAD)
L985: Parts 1 and 2 used a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled design to evaluate the PK profile of aficamten following single-ascending doses (SADs) and MADs and to describe the PK/PD relationship between aficamten and cardiac function. In Part 1, a total of 57 subjects were randomized to receive a single oral dose of aficamten at ascending dose levels of 1, 3, 10, 25, 40, 50, and 75 mg capsules (n=42) or matching placebo (n=15). All 57 subjects completed Part 1. In Part 2, a total of 24 subjects were randomized to receive oral aficamten QD at ascending dose levels of 5, 7.5, and 10 mg capsules (n=18) or matching placebo (n=6) for 14 or 17 days. All 24 subjects completed Part 2.
L986: Part 3 (CYP2D6 PM Cohort)
L987: CYP2D6 genotypes were determined at Screening. Subjects identified as CYP2D6 PMs were excluded from the SAD (i.e., Part 1) and MAD (i.e., Part 2) cohorts and instead invited to participate in the separate CYP2D6 PM cohort (i.e., Part 3). A total of 33 subjects had CYP2D6 genotype and phenotype determined: 7 PMs (i.e., *3/*4, *4/*4, *4/*5, or *4/*12), 5 intermediate metabolizers (IMs) (i.e., *1/*4, *2A/*9, or *2A/unknown), and 21 normal metabolizers (NMs) (i.e., *1/*1, *1/*2, *1/*2A, *1/*10, *1/*41, *2/*2, *2A/*2A, *2A/*41, or *2AxN/*3) were identified. Part 3 used a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, single-dose design to assess the effect of CYP2D6 genetic variants on the pharmacokinetics of aficamten. In Part 3, a total of 9 healthy CYP2D6-PMs were randomized to receive a single oral dose of aficamten 10 mg encapsulated granules capsule (n=7) or matching placebo (n=2). All 9 subjects completed Part 3.
L988: Part 4 (Food Effect) and Part 5 (Relative Bioavailability)
L989: Both parts 4 and 5 used an open-label, randomized, 2-way crossover design to evaluate the effect of a high-fat meal on the single-dose pharmacokinetics of aficamten (i.e., 1×10 mg encapsulated granules 5% [w/w] capsule) and the relative bioavailability between the initial clinical formulation (i.e., 2×5 mg encapsulated granules 5% [w/w] capsules) and the Phase 2 tablets (i.e., 2×5 mg 5% [w/w] blend tablets) formulation in healthy subjects, respectively. For Part 5, treatments were given in the fasted state and separated by a ≥14-day washout period. A total of 12 subjects were randomized and completed Part 5.
L990: The food effect cohort (i.e., Part 4) in this study was assessed using a 10-mg capsule formulation while Study CY 6012 was conducted using a 20 mg TBM tablet formulation. Therefore, Study CY 6012 will be used to assess the food effect on aficamten pharmacokinetics and Part 4 of this study will not be covered in this review.
L991: Study Results
L992: Part 1 (SAD)
L993: Plasma aficamten AUC24, AUC∞, and Cmax appeared to increase proportional to dose following single oral doses of 1, 3, 10, 25, 40, 50, and 75-mg aficamten (Figure 14 and Table 78). Median t1/2 was consistent over the dose range (i.e., 75.2 to 81.6 hours).
L994: Part 2 (MAD)
L995: Plasma aficamten AUC24 and Cmax approximately doubled with twice the dose following single oral doses of 5 and 10-mg aficamten (Figure 15 and Table 79). Similar results were observed following multiple-dose administration for 14 days. However, AUCs observed with 7.5-mg aficamten were comparable to those from 10-mg aficamten. The Applicant states that comparable exposures observed between 7.5 and 10-mg QD dosing may be due to the small sample size and variability.

L1006: Results from this study indicate no clinically relevant changes in aficamten pharmacokinetics in subjects with moderate HI versus matched control subjects with normal hepatic function. These findings can be extrapolated to subjects with mild HI, suggesting that no dose adjustment of aficamten is warranted in subjects with mild or moderate HI.
L1007: 14.2.6. Study CY 6013: Mass Balance
L1008: Study Design
L1009: This was a phase 1, open-label, nonrandomized, single-dose study to determine the mass balance of 14C-aficamten following oral administration; to characterize the pharmacokinetics of aficamten and its metabolites, CK-3834282 and CK-3834283, in plasma and urine; and to investigate the metabolite profile of 14C-aficamten in plasma, urine, and feces in healthy male subjects. A total of 8 subjects were enrolled, received a single oral dose of a 20 mg capsule containing approximately 100 µCi of 14C-aficamten, and completed the study. All subjects fasted overnight for at least 10 hours and refrained from consuming water for 1 hour predose and 2 hours postdose. Subjects fasted until approximately 4 hours postdose.
L1010: Study Results
L1011: Following administration of 14C-aficamten 20 mg, absorption of aficamten was moderate and formation of metabolites, M1a (CK-3834282) and M1b (CK-3834283) was steady with Cmax observed 3 hours later than parent (median Tmax: 5.00 versus 2.00 hours postdose). The median t½ of aficamten was 99.6 hours and similar t½ were observed for the metabolites (i.e., 94.8 and 95.4 hours), suggesting that M1a (CK-3834282) and M1b (CK-3834283) elimination is formation rate-limited (Table 100).

L1017: Source: Table 7, Module 2.7.2
L1018: Abbreviations: M, metabolite; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1019: At 600 hours postdose, the overall mean recovery of total radioactivity was 89.7% (range: 76.4% to 98.1%). Feces was the primary route of excretion and accounted for 57.7% of total radioactivity recovered, while urine accounted for 32.0% of total radioactivity recovered. Urine recovery of aficamten and metabolites, M1a (CK-3834282) and M1b (CK-3834283), was low, representing 0.06%, 5.3%, and 2.3%, respectively, of the administered dose. Corresponding renal clearance was 2.6, 443, and 105 mL/h, respectively.
L1020: Aficamten was primarily eliminated by metabolism and, to a lesser extent, by fecal excretion as unchanged aficamten (5.1%). Metabolism was mediated primarily by oxidation and reduction, with secondary pathways of glucuronidation, hydrolysis, and sulfonation (Figure 16). In plasma, the most abundant circulating components were aficamten, M1a (CK-3834282), M1b (CK-3834283), and M5 (CK-4034051), which accounted for 19.8%, 10.5%, 36.4%, and 10.3%, respectively, of total circulating radioactivity. Urinary metabolites were generally minor in abundance (i.e., &lt;10% of dose) and the only major fecal metabolite identified was M18 (CK-4017583), which accounted for 44.1% of dose.

    c. Sulfonation
    d. Biliary excretion followed by gut microbiota-mediated reduction of the 1,2,4-oxadiazole moiety

    f. Schiff-base type hydrolysis
  Biotransformation pathways of Aficamten in human. The diagram shows Aficamten (CK-3773274) being metabolized into various pathways. Pathway 'a' leads to M3 (CK-394037) and then to M1a (CK-3834282). Pathway 'b' from M1a leads to M5 (CK-4034051). Pathway 'c' from M1a leads to M7 (u) or M64 (p) (CK-4034150). Pathway 'd' from M5 leads to M18 (CK-4017583) (f). Pathway 'e' from M18 leads to M9 (CK-3941152) (u, f). Pathway 'f' from M18 also leads to M9. A box shows the chemical structures of M18 and M5 with arrows indicating the transformation steps. A legend defines the steps: a. P450-mediated hydroxylation, b. Glucuronidation, c. Sulfonation, d. Biliary excretion followed by gut microbiota-mediated reduction of the 1,2,4-oxadiazole moiety, e. Amide-type hydrolysis, f. Schiff-base type hydrolysis.
L1023: Note: Metabolite in brackets was not identified. * denotes the site of ^{14}\text{C}-label in the 1,2,4-oxadiazole moiety at the 3-position carbon atom. (p), (u), and (f) indicate presence in plasma, urine, and feces, respectively.
L1024: Source: Figure 1, Module 2.7.2
L1025: 14.2.7. Study CY 6019: Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics and Effect on QTc Interval in Healthy Subjects
L1026: Study Design
L1027: This was a randomized, double-blind, positive- and placebo-controlled, 3-way crossover design to evaluate effect of aficamten and its metabolites (i.e., CK-3834282 and CK-3834283) on the corrected value of the interval between the Q- and T-waves on the electrocardiogram tracing (QTc) interval. Subjects were randomized to treatment order and received a single oral dose of aficamten 50 mg (i.e., 10×5-mg aficamten tablets), placebo for aficamten, and 400-mg moxifloxacin (i.e., 1×400-mg tablet) separately in three treatment periods. Treatments were separated by a \geq 21-day washout period.
L1028: Study Results
L1029: Reference is made to the QT Study Review from the Interdisciplinary Review Team for Cardiac Safety Studies dated July 22, 2024 (Reference ID: 5412605) in DARRTS. The Interdisciplinary Review Team concluded that aficamten did not cause a clinically meaningful change in the QTc interval following a single 50-mg dose.
L1030: 14.2.8. Study CY 6021: Dose Finding
L1031: Study Design
L1032: This was a Phase 2, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, dose-finding study in subjects with HCM with the following key objectives: (1) to determine the safety and
L1033: tolerability of aficamten; (2) to describe the concentration- and dose-response relationship of aficamten on the resting and Valsalva LVOT-G over 10 weeks of treatment; (3) to evaluate the concentration-response relationship of aficamten on resting LVEF over 10 weeks of treatment; (4) to describe the PK/PD relationships of aficamten; and (5) to assess the effect of background HCM therapy on the PK and PD response of aficamten.
L1034: The clinical development program sought to maintain aficamten plasma concentrations required for efficacy, but with the understanding that supratherapeutic concentrations may result in systolic dysfunction, leading to decreases in stroke volume and cardiac output, compensatory increases in heart rate, and potentially clinical heart failure. Therefore, the goal of dose selection was to find the lowest possible dose to safely achieve resting and Valsalva LVOT-G targets (i.e., resting LVOT-G of &lt;30 mm Hg and Valsalva LVOT-G of &lt;50 mm Hg) and maintain normal LVEF (i.e., LVEF \geq 50\%).
L1035: Doses for the Phase 3 study were selected based on the results from subjects with oHCM enrolled in Cohorts 1 and 2 of the study and therefore, this review focuses on Cohorts 1 and 2. Cohorts 1 and 2 enrolled subjects with oHCM on background therapy including beta-blockers and/or calcium channel blockers, either as monotherapy or combined, but excluding the use of disopyramide. Subjects with symptomatic oHCM were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to aficamten or placebo and received up to 3 escalating doses of aficamten (i.e., 5, 10, and 15 mg in Cohort 1 and 10, 20, and 30 mg in Cohort 2) or placebo once daily for 10 weeks each guided by echocardiographic results. The dose titration criteria based on echocardiograms used in this study is summarized in Table 101.
L1036: Table 101. Echocardiogram Criteria for Scheduled Dose Titrations, Study CY 6021, Cohorts 1 and 2
L1037: Biplane LVEFResting LVOT-GValsalva LVOT-GActiona



&lt; 50%——Reduce doseb

\geq 50\%and&lt; 30 mmHgand&lt; 50 mmHgNo dose change

\geq 50\%and\geq 30 mmHgand/or\geq 50 mmHgIncrease dose by 5 mg
L1038: CSR = clinical study report; LVEF = left ventricular ejection fraction; LVOT-G = left ventricular outflow tract gradient.
L1039: a If none of these conditions are met, then the dose remains unchanged.
L1040: b If LVEF was &lt; 50% on the lowest dose in a cohort, the participant received placebo. If a participant's dose was down-titrated to placebo, then they remained on placebo for the duration of the study.
L1041: Source: Table 4, Module 2.7.3
L1042: There was a 4-week post-treatment follow-up period following the active treatment period. Sparse sampling for pharmacokinetics was performed in this study, and the PK data collected were used to inform the Population PK (PPK) analysis.
L1043: Study Results
L1044: The 5 mg starting dose was PD effective (i.e., had responders achieving resting and Valsalva LVOT-G targets and maintaining normal LVEF) and well-tolerated by all subjects. There were 11 out of 41 subjects in Cohorts 1 and 2 who required doses higher than 10 mg to achieve target LVOT-G (Table 8). In Cohort 1, 5 subjects were escalated to the 15-mg dose. In Cohort 2, 6
L1045: subjects were escalated to the 20-mg dose; 4 subjects achieved target gradients at this dose level (although 1 subject was down-titrated to 10 mg due to LVEF &lt;50% per site-read echocardiography), while 2 subjects were escalated further to 30 mg (Table 8). One of the 2 subjects on the 30-mg dose was down-titrated to 20 mg due to having a LVEF of 42% at Week 6 (per site-read echocardiography), and this subject also had a temporal treatment-emergent adverse event (TEAE) of peripheral edema (of moderate severity). The Applicant believes that data suggested that the 30-mg dose is not likely to add substantial clinical benefit versus risk; therefore, 20 mg was selected as the highest dose in the Phase 3 study. The Applicant's dose selection for the Phase 3 study appears to be reasonable.
L1046: 14.2.9. Trial CY 6031: Dose Proportionality and Phase 3 Trial Formulation
L1047: The Phase 3 Trial (CY 6031) was conducted using multiple units of 5-mg aficamten tablets instead of the TBM formulation of 10 mg, 15 mg, and 20-mg aficamten tablets. Therefore, the establishment of dose proportionality across the proposed TBM dose range is important.
L1048: The dose proportionality assessment of multiple QD doses of aficamten was limited to 5 mg to 10 mg in healthy subjects (Study CY 6011, Part 2) due to the limit of tolerability of LVEF reduction in this population. On April 18, 2025 (SDN: 030), the Applicant submitted the information below describing their assessment of dose proportionality for doses of 15-mg QD and 20-mg QD.
L1049: The Applicant has examined dose proportionality of aficamten across the therapeutic dose range (5-mg QD to 20-mg QD) in the Phase 3 trial (CY 6031) in subjects with oHCM using PPK analysis. A two-compartment model with linear absorption and elimination was used to describe the pharmacokinetics of aficamten. The summary of the post hoc PK estimates at different dose levels where all subjects were given the same dose of aficamten is shown in Table 102. Results demonstrate dose proportionality up to the highest clinical dose of 20-mg QD.

L1080: Table 105. Summary of Method Performance for Determination of Aficanten in Human Urine (CA27882-01)
L1081: Bioanalytical Method Validation Report Name, Amendments, and Hyperlinks
Validation of an LC-MS/MS Method for the Determination of CK-3773274 in Isopropanol (IPA)-Treated Human Urine
Method Validation Report CA27882-01 Amendment 1


Method Description
                An aliquot of IPA-treated human urine containing the analyte and internal standard are extracted using a liquid-liquid extraction procedure. The extracted samples are analyzed using Waters Acquity UPLC Binary Solvent Manager pump, Waters Acquity UPLC I-Class System (SM-FTN) autosampler, and AB SCIEX API 5000 triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using an ESI source. Positive ions are monitored in the MRM mode.
            

Materials Used for Standard Calibration Curve and Concentration

L1089: Bioanalytical Method Validation Report Name, Amendments, and Hyperlinks
Validation of an LC-MS/MS Method for the Determination of CK-3834282 and CK-3834283 in Human Plasma (EDTA)
        Method Validation Report CA29736-01 Amendment 3


Method Description
        An aliquot of human plasma (EDTA) containing the analyte and internal standard is extracted using a liquid-liquid extraction procedure. The extracted samples are analyzed by Waters Acquity UPLC 1-Class System (SM-FTN) equipped with AB SCIEX Triple Quad 6500 triple quadrupole mass spectrometer using an ESI source. Positive ions are monitored in the MRM mode.

L1092: 22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of Scientific Investigations, Other Inspections)
L1093: The Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI) conducted inspections covering the pivotal Phase 3 Trial CY 6031. Please refer to the OSI review dated July 29, 2025, for details. A summary of the review is provided below:
L1094: OSI inspected two clinical investigator sites and the Applicant, Cytokinetics Inc. Dr. Ahmad Masri's site (Site 1001) in Portland, Oregon, screened 29 subjects and randomized 20 subjects, while Dr. Michael Nassif's site (Site 1009) in Kansas City, Missouri, screened 18 subjects and randomized 12 subjects. All randomized subjects at both sites completed the study. The inspections verified the primary efficacy endpoint data (pVO2) and found no discrepancies between the Applicant's data listings and the sites' source documents.
L1095: The overall assessment concluded that the study appears to have been conducted adequately, and the data generated by the clinical investigator sites and submitted by the Applicant appear acceptable in support of the proposed indication. The Applicant's conduct and oversight of the study were found to be adequate and compliant with Good Clinical Practice and FDA regulations.
L1096: 23. Labeling: Key Changes
L1097: This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 137). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L1098: Table 137. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations
L1099: Full PI Sections1Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI



BOXED WARNINGThe Boxed Warning was revised for consistency with current labeling practices to note that MYQORZO is available through a restricted program under a REMS.

1 INDICATIONS AND USAGEThe indication statement was revised to include the demonstrated benefits, 'to improve functional capacity and symptoms.'

2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATIONThis section was revised to improve clarity regarding recommendations as to when to perform echocardiogram assessments.Instructions regarding concomitant use with Fluconazole, Voriconazole, Fluvoxamine, Strong CYP2C9 Inhibitors, and moderate to strong CYP3A inducers. See Section 8.2.

4 CONTRAINDICATIONSContraindication for use with (b) (4) was removed.
L1100: Full PI Sections1Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI



5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS
A subsection for the MYQORZO REMS was added.
In the cytochrome P450 interactions warning, CYP2C19 was included as contributing to the metabolism of MYQORZO.
Instructions were also added to assess LVEF 2 to 8 weeks after initiation of inhibitors or discontinuation of inducers and to adjust the dose of MYQORZO accordingly.


6 ADVERSE REACTIONS
A description of effects on blood pressure was added to Section 6.1.


7 DRUG INTERACTIONS
Instructions for mitigation of drug interactions with Fluvoxamine, Fluconazole, Voriconazole, Strong CYP2C9 inhibitors and CYP inducers were revised. See Section 8.2
A proposed subsection regarding interactions with drugs with negative inotropic effects was deleted as no clinically significant interactions were noted.


8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS (e.g., Pregnancy, Lactation, Females and Males of Reproductive Potential, Pediatric Use, Geriatric Use, Renal Impairment, Hepatic Impairment)
The Pregnancy and Lactations subsections were revised for consistency with current Pregnancy Lactation and Labeling Rule, (PLLR) practices. Language describing the pregnancy safety study was added.


9 DRUG ABUSE AND DEPENDENCEN/A

10 OVERDOSAGE
This section was revised to note potential signs and symptoms of overdosage and to add language advising contacting the Poison Help line.


12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
The Mechanism of Action subsection, 12.1 was revised to focus the description of the mechanism determined most relevant to the indication.
In the Pharmacodynamics subsection, p-values for described effects were deleted.
In the Pharmacokinetics subsection, the predicted time to steady state was added. Descriptions of drug interactions sections were revised, please refer to Section 8.2 of this review.


13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY
The Pharmacokinetics subsection was also generally revised for consistency with current labeling practices.
A pharmacogenomics sections was added to address CYP2C9 genetic variants. See Section 14.6.
The applicant proposed subsection for Animal Toxicology and/or Pharmacology was deleted, this section was determined to not be clinically relevant.


14 CLINICAL STUDIES
Clinical Studies was revised to clearly describe the data supporting the indication. Analyses and presentation of the data were revised consistent with the Team's review, see Section 6.3.2.
(b) (4)was removed.


17 PATIENT COUNSELING INFORMATION
Language regarding the MYQORZO REMS and the presence of the Pregnancy Safety Study was added.

L1107: 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1108: • Descriptive Pregnancy Safety Study: Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in women exposed to aficamten during pregnancy to assess risk of pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate, and adverse effects on the infant. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life.• Lactation Study: Perform a milk-only lactation study in lactating women who have received aficamten to measure concentrations of aficamten in breast milk using a validated assay. Assess the effects on the breastfed infant, if available, based on study population.

L1203: Summary
L1204: In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in Sprague Dawley rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of CK-3773274, when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
L1205: Rat Study:
L1206: Survival analysis:
L1207: 1. For male rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups (p-value of likelihood ratio test is &lt;0.0001 and p-value of log-rank test is &lt;0.0001). The pairwise comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is &lt;0.0001 and p-value of log-rank test is &lt;0.0001).2. For female rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups (p-value of likelihood ratio test is 0.0014 and p-value of log-rank test is 0.0014). The pairwise comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is 0.0288 and p-value of log-rank test is 0.0263).
L1208: Tumor analysis:
L1209: 1. For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and each of the treated groups.2. For female rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant difference in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and the low dose group (p-value=0.0221).
L1210: 1. Background
L1211: In this submission the sponsor included the report of one animal carcinogenicity study in Sprague Dawley rats. This study was intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of CK-3773274, when administered by oral gavage once daily for 104 weeks.
L1212: In this review the phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as the dose increases.
L1213: Rat Study
L1214: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in males and one in females. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups and one vehicle control group. Two hundred and sixty rats of each sex were randomly assigned to the treated groups and vehicle control group in equal size of 65 rats per group. The study was designed to continue for up to 104 weeks for both sexes.
L1215: Due to lower survival (n=20) in males and females administered 2 and 3 mg/kg/day, respectively, dose administration of all surviving males and females at these dose levels was suspended as of Days 474/475 (Week 68) and 635/636 (Week 91), respectively.
L1216: Animals were maintained on study, without dosing and due to continued low survivability (n=15), all surviving males at 2 mg/kg/day were euthanized during Weeks 79 and 80, and surviving females at 3 mg/kg/day were euthanized during Week 96. Due to lower survival (n=20) in control males and females, all surviving males were euthanized during Weeks 100 and 101, and all surviving females were euthanized during Week 103.
L1217: Table 1: Study Design in Rat Study
L1218: Protocol Group No.Dose Levels (mg/kg/day) Male/FemaleIdentificationNumber of Animals Enrolled

MalesFemales



10Vehicle6565

20.5Low6565

L1219: 1.1. Sponsor's analyses
L1220: 1.1.1. Survival analysis
L1221: Kaplan-Meier estimates of group survival rates were calculated by sex and shown graphically.
L1222: A log-rank dose response trend test of survival rates was performed utilizing ordinal coefficients. In addition, a log-rank test for survival was used to make pairwise comparisons of each treated group with the vehicle control group. Both the trend test and pairwise comparisons were conducted at the 0.05 significance level.
L1223: Survival times in which the status of the animal's death was classified as terminal euthanasia were considered censored values for the purpose of the Kaplan-Meier estimates and survival rate analyses.
L1224: Sponsor's findings: The sponsor's analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival were 19 (29%), 17 (26%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) male rats, and 20 (31%), 24 (38%), 20 (31%), and 15 (23%) in female rats in vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, respectively.
L1225: The sponsor concluded that there was a statistically significant lower survivability rate in males administered 2 mg/kg/day and females administered 3 mg/kg/day, compared with controls as well as a significant decreasing trend across dose groups.
L1226: 1.1.2. Tumor data analysis
L1227: The incidence of tumours at different dose levels were analysed using a Peto analysis with unadjusted p-values, following the recommendations of OECD guidance document 116.
L1228: Tumours were distinguished by their finding (e.g., carcinoma) and their organ of occurrence. All of the tumours distinguished by finding and organ as contained in the data were analysed. A time stratum was used in the Peto analysis for the prevalence part: The experimental period was divided into the following time intervals : 0-52 weeks, 53-78 weeks, 79-92 weeks, over 92 weeks, and planned (terminal) sacrifice. These time intervals were applied to time to occurrence for incidental tumours. The occurrence part for fatal tumours of the Peto analysis used days as unit, using day of occurrence for fatal tumours. If day of occurrence was not available, day of death was used instead.
L1229: As described in, the methodology used normal approximations which may be unreliable if the number of tumours is small. For data sets with 10 tumours or fewer, a permutation test was used, as implemented in SAS Version 9.4 for PROC MULTTEST. The permutation test has also been used for data sets of 11 or more tumours.
L1230: Analyses were carried out by sex. Active treatment groups (i.e., Groups 2, 3, and 4) were compared with the control group (i.e., Group 1) using Petos one-sided test for trends, testing for higher incidence in the active treatment groups.
L1231: In addition to the linear trend test for all dose groups, pairwise comparisons of active treatment groups with the control group were carried out as well, using Peto one-sided test.
L1232: Sponsor's findings: In conclusion, there were no statistically significant tumor findings after CK-3773274 administration to rats by oral gavage once daily for up to 704 days at doses of up to 2 mg/kg/day in males and for up to 721 days at doses of up to 3 mg/kg/day in females.
L1233: 1.2. Reviewer's analyses
L1234: To verify sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.
L1235: 1.2.1. Survival analysis
L1236: The survival distributions of three treated groups, one vehicular control group, and one positive
L1237: control group were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose response relationship in survival was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1 and 2 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 6 and 7 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals among the vehicle control and three treated groups are given in Tables 8 and 9 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L1238: Reviewer's findings: The reviewer's analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival were 19 (29%), 17 (26%), 16 (25%), and 15 (23%) male rats, and 20 (31%), 24 (38%), 20 (31%), and 15 (23%) in female rats in vehicle control, low, mid and high dose groups, respectively.
L1239: For male rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups (p-value of likelihood ratio test is &lt;0.0001 and p-value of log-rank test is &lt;0.0001). The pairwise comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is &lt;0.0001 and p-value of log-rank test is &lt;0.0001).
L1240: For female rats, the survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality across the vehicle control group and treated groups (p-value of likelihood ratio test is 0.0014 and p-value of log-rank test is 0.0014). The pairwise comparison showed a statistically positive significant difference in mortality between the vehicle control group and the high dose group (p-value of likelihood ratio test is 0.0288 and p-value of log-rank test is 0.0263).

L1245: 1.2.2. Tumor data analysis
L1246: The tumor data were analyzed for the positive dose response relationships and the positive pairwise comparison increases between each of the treated groups with control group. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-K method described in the paper of Bailer and Portier (1988) and Bieler and Williams (1993). In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without a tumor before the end of the study gets a score of
L1247: s_h = \left( \frac{w_h}{w_{max}} \right)^k &lt; 1. \text{ The adjusted group size is defined as } \sum s_h. \text{ As an interpretation, an animal}
L1248: with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes were then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104 week standard rat and rat studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
L1249: The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons between vehicle control and three treated groups are listed in Tables 10, and 11 in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L1250: Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study, the more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.005 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.025 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study. For pairwise comparisons for the chronic rat study in the above type of submission with one chronic rat study, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels \alpha = 0.01 for common tumors and \alpha = 0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic rat study.
L1251: It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008) showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for Poly-K tests.
L1252: Reviewer's findings:
L1253: For male rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated groups. The
L1254: pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and each of the treated groups.
L1255: For female rats, the tumor analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidence across the vehicle control groups and treated groups. The pairwise comparisons showed a statistically significant difference in tumor incidence between the vehicle control groups and the low dose group (p\text{-value}=0.0221).

L1287: Figure 1: Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Male Rats
L1288: The figure is a Kaplan-Meier survival plot titled "Product-Limit Survival Estimates". The y-axis is labeled "Survival Probability" and ranges from 0.0 to 1.0. The x-axis is labeled "Time in week of death or sacrifice" and ranges from 0 to 100. There are four survival curves: 0.5 mkd (blue), 1 mkd (red), 2 mkd (teal), and Vehicle Control (brown). The Vehicle Control curve remains at 1.0 until week 25, then drops to approximately 0.9 by week 40, and continues to decline to about 0.25 by week 100. The 1 mkd curve follows a similar pattern but declines more sharply after week 60, reaching about 0.25 by week 100. The 0.5 mkd curve shows a more gradual decline, reaching about 0.3 by week 100. The 2 mkd curve shows the steepest decline, dropping to about 0.25 by week 80. L1290: Figure 2: Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Female Rats
L1291: The figure is a Kaplan-Meier survival plot titled "Product-Limit Survival Estimates". The y-axis is labeled "Survival Probability" and ranges from 0.0 to 1.0. The x-axis is labeled "Time in week of death or sacrifice" and ranges from 0 to 100. There are four survival curves representing different dose groups: 0.5 mkd (blue), 1 mkd (red), 3 mkd (teal), and Vehicle Control (brown). The Vehicle Control group shows 100% survival throughout the period. The 0.5 mkd, 1 mkd, and 3 mkd groups show a decrease in survival probability over time, with the 3 mkd group showing the lowest survival rate by week 100.
